🧪 SCENARIO S21 — Category 2: Network Policy

Debug CiliumNetworkPolicy — Empty endpointSelector Blocks All Pods

The Problem: You applied a CiliumNetworkPolicy to the anihpj namespace. Suddenly ALL pods lose ALL network connectivity — no ingress, no egress, no DNS. Hubble shows every flow as DROPPED (Policy Denied).
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-web-to-api
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {}            # ❌ BUG: matches ALL pods!
  egress:
  - toEndpoints:
    - matchLabels:
        app: api
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pods Running: kubectl get pods -n anihpj → web+api Running ✅
✗2. ALL traffic DROPPED: hubble observe -n anihpj → every flow DROPPED ❌
✗3. DNS broken: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- nslookup kubernetes.default → timeout ❌
✗4. CNP exists: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj → allow-web-to-api
✗5. Describe shows empty selector: kubectl describe cnp -n anihpj → endpointSelector: {}
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Inspect CNP selector: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-web-to-api -o yaml | grep -A3 endpointSelector
→ endpointSelector: {} — matches ALL pods!
2Check which pods are selected: cilium policy selectors -n anihpj
→ Both web AND api pods selected by the same empty rule
3Verify egress-only rule: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-web-to-api -o yaml | grep -c ingress
→ 0 ingress rules — implicit deny-all on ingress!
4Hubble confirms universal drops: hubble observe -n anihpj --verdict DROPPED → Every pod flow DROPPED
5Root cause identified: Empty {} selector + egress-only rule = all pods selected with ingress denied
🔧

🔧 Fix — Narrow the endpointSelector to Only Web Pods

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-web-to-api --type merge -p '{"spec":{"endpointSelector":{"matchLabels":{"app":"web"}}}}'
✓

✅ Verify — Network Restored, Only Web Selected

After patching the CNP with matchLabels: {app: web}, only web pods are selected by the policy. API pods regain full connectivity. hubble observe shows FORWARDED flows from web to api. The key lesson: endpointSelector: {} is a cluster-wide footgun — always scope with matchLabels.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Empty selector matches all pods
②
Hubble shows universal DROPS
③
CNP YAML confirms {} selector
④
Add matchLabels to narrow scope
⑤
Verify: only web pods selected

Tenet: An empty endpointSelector: {} in a CiliumNetworkPolicy matches every pod in the namespace. Egress-only rules imply default-deny on ingress. Always scope endpointSelector with matchLabels.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 wget: download timed out ← All pod-to-pod blocked!
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --verdict DROPPED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:01:01 anihpj/web-xxx anihpj/api-xxx DROPPED 12:01:02 anihpj/web-yyy 10.96.0.10:53 DROPPED ← Even DNS blocked! {} selects web AND api
$ cilium policy selectors -n anihpj RULE: allow-web-to-api endpointSelector: {} ← Matches BOTH web and api pods!
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --verdict FORWARDED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:05:01 anihpj/web-xxx anihpj/api-xxx FORWARDED ✅ 12:05:02 anihpj/web-yyy anihpj/api-yyy FORWARDED ✅
$ cilium policy selectors -n anihpj RULE: allow-web-to-api endpointSelector: app=web ✅ Only web pods selected
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/api -- wget -qO- http://10.0.1.10:80 HTTP 200 OK ✅ API pod unaffected by web-only policy
🧹

🧹 Cleanup

BASH
kubectl delete namespace anihpj
S22
🧪 SCENARIO S22 — Category 2: Network Policy

Debug L7 Policy — HTTP Path Regex Not Matching Sub-Paths

The Problem: You created a CNP with L7 HTTP to allow GET /api/v1/. But GET /api/v1/health is DROPPED while GET /api/v1/ works. Hubble shows the HTTP path correctly but the regex doesn't match.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
kubectl expose deployment api -n anihpj --port=80
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-api-readonly
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints:
    - matchLabels: {app: web}
    toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "80", protocol: TCP}]
      rules:
        http:
        - method: GET
          path: "^/api/v1/$"           # ❌ BUG: $ anchors exact — /health fails!
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pods Running ✅, CNP exists ✅
✗2. GET /api/v1/health blocked: curl http://api-service/api/v1/health → 403 ❌
✗3. GET /api/v1/ works: curl http://api-service/api/v1/ → 200 OK ✅
✗4. Hubble: hubble observe -n anihpj --http-method GET → /health DROPPED ❌
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Inspect CNP path regex: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-api-readonly -o yaml | grep path
→ path: "^/api/v1/$" — $ anchors end-of-string!
2Hubble HTTP detail: hubble observe -n anihpj -o json | jq '.flow.l7.http'
→ Request URL: /api/v1/health, verdict: DROPPED
3Test regex manually: echo '/api/v1/health' | grep -E '^/api/v1/$'
→ NO MATCH! Regex $ prevents sub-path matching
4Compare with working path: echo '/api/v1/' | grep -E '^/api/v1/$' → MATCHES (exact only)
5Root cause identified: Regex $ anchor forces exact match — sub-paths like /api/v1/health don't match
🔧

🔧 Fix — Remove $ Anchor for Prefix Matching

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-api-readonly --type merge -p '{"spec":{"ingress":[{"fromEndpoints":[{"matchLabels":{"app":"web"}}],"toPorts":[{"ports":[{"port":"80","protocol":"TCP"}],"rules":{"http":[{"method":"GET","path":"^/api/v1/"}]}}]}]}}'
✓

✅ Verify — All /api/v1/* Sub-Paths Now Match

After removing the $ anchor, the regex ^/api/v1/ matches ALL sub-paths. curl /api/v1/health returns 200. hubble observe shows FORWARDED for all /api/v1/* requests. The key lesson: $ in regex anchors to end-of-string — omit it for prefix matching.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
HTTP path blocked → check regex
②
Hubble shows DROPPED with path
③
Test regex: $ anchors end-of-string
④
Remove $ → prefix match works
⑤
All /api/v1/* paths now match

Tenet: L7 HTTP path rules use regex. $ anchors to end-of-string — ^/api/v1/$ only matches exactly /api/v1/. For prefix matching, omit $. Always test your regex against real paths.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://api-service/api/v1/health 403 ← Forbidden by L7!
$ hubble observe -n anihpj -o json | jq '.flow.l7.http.url, .flow.verdict' "http://api-service/api/v1/health" "DROPPED" ← HTTP path regex mismatch
$ echo '/api/v1/health' | grep -E '^/api/v1/$' (no output) ← Regex mismatch confirmed
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://api-service/api/v1/health 200 ✅ Sub-path now matches
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --http-path /api/v1/ --verdict FORWARDED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION HTTP VERDICT 12:08:01 web-xxx api-service GET /api/v1/health FORWARDED ✅

Debug toFQDNs Policy — DNS Resolution Blocked Before FQDN Match

The Problem: You created a CNP with toFQDNs to allow egress to api.github.com. But DNS resolution itself is blocked. Hubble shows UDP/53 DROPPED before FQDN rule can activate.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-github
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: web}}
  egress:
  - toFQDNs: [{matchName: "api.github.com"}]       # ❌ BUG: FQDN needs DNS first!
  - toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "443", protocol: TCP}]}]
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pod Running ✅, CNP exists ✅
✗2. DNS blocked: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- nslookup api.github.com → timeout ❌
✗3. Hubble DNS: hubble observe -n anihpj --port 53 → DROPPED ❌
✗4. HTTPS blocked: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget --timeout=3 https://api.github.com → timeout ❌
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check DNS traffic: hubble observe -n anihpj --port 53 --verdict DROPPED
→ All UDP/53 traffic to kube-dns is DROPPED!
2Inspect policy ports: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-github -o yaml | grep -c 'port.*53'
→ 0 — no DNS port rule in the policy!
3Understand toFQDNs flow: Cilium must see DNS response to map IP→FQDN identity → DNS must be allowed BEFORE the FQDN rule
4Check FQDN rule: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -A3 toFQDNs → matchName: api.github.com exists but never activates
5Root cause identified: toFQDNs rule requires DNS first — policy has no UDP/53 allow rule for kube-dns
🔧

🔧 Fix — Add DNS Egress Rule Before FQDN Rule

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-github --type json -p '[{"op":"add","path":"/spec/egress/-","value":{"toEndpoints":[{"matchLabels":{"k8s-app":"kube-dns","io.kubernetes.pod.namespace":"kube-system"}}],"toPorts":[{"ports":[{"port":"53","protocol":"UDP"}]}]}}]'
✓

✅ Verify — DNS Resolves, FQDN-Based Egress Works

After adding the UDP/53 egress rule to kube-dns, DNS resolution succeeds. nslookup api.github.com returns the IP. Cilium's DNS proxy maps the IP to the FQDN identity and the toFQDNs rule activates. hubble observe shows FORWARDED HTTPS traffic. The key lesson: toFQDNs ALWAYS needs a DNS allow rule first.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
toFQDNs blocks → DNS needed
②
Hubble DNS DROPPED
③
Policy lacks UDP/53
④
Add DNS allow rule
⑤
DNS resolves → FQDN works

Tenet: toFQDNs rules require DNS to map FQDNs to IPs, but Cilium enforces policies BEFORE DNS. You MUST explicitly allow egress UDP/53 to kube-dns. Without it, the pod can't resolve and the toFQDNs rule never activates.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- nslookup api.github.com ;; connection timed out; no servers could be reached ← DNS BLOCKED
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --port 53 --verdict DROPPED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION PORT VERDICT 12:01:01 web-xxx 10.96.0.10 53 DROPPED ← DNS blocked before FQDN rule can match
$ kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -c 'port.*53' 0 ← No DNS port rule at all
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix

$ hubble observe -n anihpj --port 53 --verdict FORWARDED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION PORT VERDICT 12:06:01 web-xxx 10.96.0.10 53 FORWARDED ✅

Debug Ingress Policy — Cross-Namespace Traffic Blocked by Missing Namespace Label

1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create namespace frontend
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
kubectl expose deployment api -n anihpj --port=80
kubectl create deployment web -n frontend --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-from-frontend
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints:
    - matchLabels:
        app: web          # ❌ BUG: only matches in same namespace!
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pods Running in both namespaces ✅
✗2. Cross-ns blocked: kubectl exec -n frontend deploy/web -- wget --timeout=3 http://api-service.anihpj:80 → timeout ❌
✗3. Hubble: hubble observe -n anihpj --from-namespace frontend → DROPPED ❌
✗4. Same-ns test: deploy web in anihpj → works ✅ (selector matches within same namespace)
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Inspect CNP fromEndpoints: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-from-frontend -o yaml | grep -A5 fromEndpoints
→ matchLabels: {app: web} — no namespace selector!
2Check source namespace in Hubble: hubble observe -n anihpj -o json | jq '.flow.source.namespace'
→ frontend — but policy defaults to same-ns (anihpj)
3Verify CNP namespace scope: CNP selectors are namespace-scoped — app: web only matches pods in anihpj
4Cross-ns needs explicit label: Must add io.kubernetes.pod.namespace: frontend to the selector

🔧

🔧 Fix — Add Namespace Label to Cross-Namespace Selector

BASH

After adding io.kubernetes.pod.namespace: frontend to the fromEndpoints selector, the CNP now matches pods in the frontend namespace. wget from frontend/web to anihpj/api succeeds. hubble observe shows FORWARDED cross-namespace flows. The key lesson: CNP selectors are namespace-scoped by default — always add namespace label for cross-ns traffic.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Cross-ns traffic DROPPED
②
Hubble → ns mismatch
③
No namespace in selector
④
Add io.kubernetes.pod.namespace
⑤
Cross-ns traffic flows

Tenet: CiliumNetworkPolicy selectors are namespace-scoped by default. matchLabels: {app: web} only matches pods in the same namespace. For cross-namespace traffic, add io.kubernetes.pod.namespace: <target-ns>.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)

$ kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -A5 fromEndpoints - fromEndpoints: - matchLabels: app: web ← Only matches in anihpj namespace!
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ kubectl exec -n frontend deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://api-service.anihpj:80 HTTP 200 OK ✅ Cross-namespace traffic allowed

  namespace: anihpj

→ toEntities: [world] — 'world' = ALL external destinations!

→ world=reserved:world(2) = complete internet, cluster=reserved:cluster(1) = internal only
3Verify Hubble flows: hubble observe -n anihpj --to-entity world → All external traffic FORWARDED

Internal cluster traffic still works. hubble observe shows no flows to world entity. The key lesson: toEntities: [world] = unrestricted internet access — use [cluster] for internal-only.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①

②
Entity = world not cluster
③
world = internet access
④
Change to cluster
⑤
Internal only ✅

Tenet: toEntities: [world] allows egress to ALL destinations outside the cluster. For internal-only traffic, use toEntities: [cluster]. Entity names: world (external), cluster (internal), host (node), init, unmanaged, health.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)

📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix

③

④

1

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj

  name: deny-all-egress
  namespace: anihpj

  - toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "53", protocol: UDP}]}]
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pod Running ✅, CNP exists ✅
✗2. External access WORKS (shouldn't!): kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget --timeout=3 http://1.1.1.1 → Connected ❌
✗3. Hubble shows FORWARDED: hubble observe -n anihpj --to-entity world → FORWARDED ❌
✗4. Policy status: cilium policy get → audit mode
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check enforcement mode: cilium endpoint list
→ POLICY ENFORCEMENT: false — policy evaluated but NOT enforced!
2Check policy mode: cilium policy get | head -5
→ Policy Enforcement Mode: audit — audit mode active!
3Hubble match type: hubble observe -n anihpj -o json | jq '.flow.policy_match_type'
→ "audit" — policy matched but not enforced
4Audit mode behavior: Policy is evaluated and reported in Hubble, but traffic passes through — useful for testing before enforcement
5Root cause identified: PolicyEnforcementMode set to 'audit' — policy rules are evaluated but traffic is NOT blocked
🔧

🔧 Fix — Switch from Audit Mode to Enforcement Mode

BASH
cilium config PolicyEnforcementMode=default
kubectl delete cnp -n anihpj deny-all-egress
kubectl apply -f - << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: deny-all-egress
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: web}}
  egress:
  - toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "53", protocol: UDP}]}]
CNP
✓

✅ Verify — Egress Now Enforced, External Traffic Blocked

After switching PolicyEnforcementMode to default and re-applying the CNP, egress restrictions are now enforced. wget http://1.1.1.1 times out. hubble observe shows DROPPED external traffic. The key lesson: audit mode = policy evaluated but NOT enforced — use 'default' for real enforcement.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Traffic allowed → surprising
②
Hubble → FORWARDED (should deny)
③
Check policy → audit mode
④
Audit = evaluate, not enforce
⑤
Switch to default → enforced

Tenet: Cilium policies can run in audit mode — the policy is evaluated and visible in Hubble, but NOT enforced. Traffic passes through. Useful for testing. Ensure PolicyEnforcementMode is 'default' or 'always' for real enforcement.

Debug fromCIDR — Wrong Subnet Mask Blocks Legitimate Traffic

The Problem: You added fromCIDR: 10.10.0.0/24 for VPN, but users on 10.10.1.50 can't reach the API. Your actual VPN is /16.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
kubectl expose deployment api -n anihpj --port=80

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-vpn
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromCIDR: [10.10.0.0/24]       # ❌ BUG: /24 = only 256 IPs!
    toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "80", protocol: TCP}]}]
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pod Running ✅, CNP exists ✅
✗2. VPN user 10.10.1.50 blocked: curl --connect-to api-service:80:10.0.1.20:80 http://api-service → timeout ❌
✗3. VPN user 10.10.0.50 works: IP within /24 works ✅
✗4. Hubble CIDR: hubble observe -n anihpj --from-ip 10.10.1.50 → DROPPED ❌
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Inspect CNP CIDR: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-vpn -o yaml | grep fromCIDR
→ fromCIDR: 10.10.0.0/24 — only 256 IPs!
2Calculate CIDR range: python3 -c "import ipaddress; print(list(ipaddress.IPv4Network('10.10.0.0/24'))[0], '→', list(ipaddress.IPv4Network('10.10.0.0/24'))[-1])"
→ 10.10.0.0 → 10.10.0.255 — 10.10.1.50 is OUTSIDE!
3Check actual VPN range: /24 = 256 IPs, /16 = 65,536 IPs — VPN subnet is /16
4Verify boundary: IP 10.10.0.50 is inside /24 and works; IP 10.10.1.50 is outside and fails
5Root cause identified: CIDR /24 mask only covers 256 IPs — VPN uses /16 which covers 65,536 IPs across all subnets
🔧

🔧 Fix — Expand fromCIDR from /24 to /16

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-vpn --type merge -p '{"spec":{"ingress":[{"fromCIDR":["10.10.0.0/16"],"toPorts":[{"ports":[{"port":"80","protocol":"TCP"}]}]}]}}'
✓

✅ Verify — Full VPN Range Now Covered

After expanding fromCIDR to 10.10.0.0/16, all VPN users in the 10.10.x.x range can reach the API. curl from 10.10.1.50 returns HTTP 200. hubble observe shows FORWARDED for the full VPN range. The key lesson: CIDR mask determines IP range — /24 = 256, /16 = 65,536 — always match your actual subnet.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
VPN partial access → check CIDR
②
CIDR = /24 = 256 IPs only
③
/16 covers full VPN range
④
IP 10.10.1.50 is outside /24
⑤
Expand to /16 → works

Tenet: fromCIDR uses standard CIDR notation. /24 = 256 addresses, /16 = 65,536. Always verify subnet mask matches actual network range. Test boundary IPs to confirm coverage.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)

$ hubble observe -n anihpj --from-ip 10.10.1.50 TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:01:01 10.10.1.50:45678 api-xxx:80 DROPPED ← Source IP not matching fromCIDR 10.10.0.0/24

Debug ICMP Policy — Ping Blocked by Missing ICMP Rule

The Problem: You applied a CNP allowing only TCP/80 and UDP/53. Now ping/traceroute to pods fail. ICMP is a separate protocol (1) that must be explicitly allowed.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-web-to-api
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {app: web}}]
    toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "80", protocol: TCP}, {port: "53", protocol: UDP}]
      # ❌ BUG: No ICMP rule!
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pods Running ✅, TCP/80 works: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget http://api-ip:80 → 200 OK ✅
✗2. Ping blocked: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- ping -c3 -W2 api-ip → 100% loss ❌
✗3. Traceroute blocked: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- traceroute api-ip → all * * * ❌
✗4. Hubble ICMP: hubble observe -n anihpj --protocol ICMPv4 → DROPPED ❌
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check allowed protocols: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-web-to-api -o yaml | grep protocol
→ Only TCP and UDP listed — no ICMP rule!
2Verify protocol numbers: ICMP=1, TCP=6, UDP=17 — each is a separate IP protocol
3Hubble ICMP check: hubble observe -n anihpj --protocol ICMPv4 --verdict DROPPED
→ All ICMP Echo Request/Reply traffic is DROPPED
4Impact of missing ICMP: Ping, traceroute, PMTUD (Path MTU Discovery) all broken — can cause hard-to-diagnose issues
5Root cause identified: ICMP (protocol 1) is not listed in the policy — Cilium implicitly denies unlisted protocols
🔧

🔧 Fix — Add ICMP Rule (Port 8 Echo Request)

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-web-to-api --type json -p '[{"op":"add","path":"/spec/ingress/0/toPorts/-","value":{"ports":[{"port":"8","protocol":"ICMP"}]}}]'
✓

✅ Verify — Ping and Traceroute Work

After adding the ICMP rule (port 8 for Echo Request), ping succeeds with 0% packet loss. hubble observe shows FORWARDED ICMPv4 traffic. The key lesson: ICMP is a separate protocol (1) from TCP (6) and UDP (17) — it must be explicitly allowed in Cilium policies.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Ping fails but TCP works
②
ICMP is separate protocol
③
Policy only has TCP+UDP
④
Missing ICMP → denied
⑤
Add ICMP → ping works

Tenet: ICMP (protocol 1) is separate from TCP (6) and UDP (17). Cilium policy rules are protocol-specific. If your policy only lists TCP/UDP, ALL ICMP traffic is implicitly denied. Add ICMP rule (port 8 for Echo Request) for ping/traceroute/PMTUD.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- ping -c3 -W2 10.0.1.20 PING 10.0.1.20: 56 data bytes --- 10.0.1.20 ping statistics --- 3 packets transmitted, 0 received, 100% loss ← ICMP blocked!
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --protocol ICMPv4 --verdict DROPPED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION PROTO VERDICT 12:01:01 web-xxx api-xxx ICMPv4 DROPPED ← ICMP not in policy → denied
$ kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep protocol protocol: TCP protocol: UDP ← No ICMP rule!
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- ping -c3 -W2 10.0.1.20 PING 10.0.1.20: 56 data bytes 64 bytes from 10.0.1.20: icmp_seq=0 ttl=64 time=0.1ms --- 3 packets transmitted, 3 received, 0% loss ✅
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --protocol ICMPv4 --verdict FORWARDED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION PROTO VERDICT 12:05:01 web-xxx api-xxx ICMPv4 FORWARDED ✅

BASH

  name: require-api-key
  namespace: anihpj
spec:

    toPorts:

③
Verify YAML formatting
④
Re-apply clean policy
⑤

Tenet: L7 HTTP header rules use exact string matching in format Header-Name: value. YAML parsing, encoding, or invisible characters can corrupt the match. Always verify with Hubble that headers in requests match policy exactly.

# ❌ BUG: CCNP with empty ingress = deny ALL ingress cluster-wide!

apiVersion: cilium.io/v2

apiVersion: cilium.io/v2

⑤

Tenet: CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy applies across ALL namespaces. Rules are additive — if either CNP or CCNP denies, traffic is denied. CCNP with empty ingress: [] creates cluster-wide deny-all. Use CCNP carefully with scoped selectors.

🧪 SCENARIO S33 — Category 2: Network Policy

Debug Rule Order — Multiple Ingress Rules Are Additive (OR Logic)

The Problem: You have a CNP with two ingress rules: one for web, one for other. But BOTH web AND other can reach api — you expected 'other' to be denied. Rules are additive, not sequential.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600
kubectl create deployment other -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-web-only
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {app: web}}]
  - fromEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {app: other}}]    # ❌ BUG: Second rule ALSO allows!
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pods Running ✅
✗2. Web→api works: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget http://api-ip:80 → 200 OK ✅
✗3. Other→api WORKS (shouldn't!): kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/other -- wget http://api-ip:80 → 200 OK ❌
✗4. Hubble: both sources FORWARDED
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Count ingress rules: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-web-only -o yaml | grep -c fromEndpoints
→ 2 — two separate ingress rules exist!
2Inspect rule 1: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -A3 'fromEndpoints:' | head -5
→ matchLabels: {app: web} — allows web pods
3Inspect rule 2: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -A3 'fromEndpoints:' | tail -5
→ matchLabels: {app: other} — ALSO allows other pods!
4Understand Cilium logic: Multiple ingress rules combine with OR logic — traffic matching ANY rule is allowed
5Root cause identified: Two ingress rules are additive (OR logic) — the second rule doesn't override the first, it adds another allowed source
🔧

🔧 Fix — Remove the Second Ingress Rule

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-web-only --type json -p '[{"op":"remove","path":"/spec/ingress/1"}]'
✓

✅ Verify — Only Web Pods Can Reach API

After removing the second ingress rule, only web pods can reach the API. wget from 'other' pod times out. hubble observe shows FORWARDED for web but DROPPED for other. The key lesson: Cilium ingress rules use OR logic — multiple rules are additive, not sequential.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Both web+other allowed
②
Two rules = OR logic
③
Both are ALLOW rules
④
Remove second rule
⑤
Only web now allowed

Tenet: Cilium policy ingress rules are additive (OR logic) — multiple rules combine, and traffic matching ANY rule is allowed. A second fromEndpoints rule doesn't override the first; it adds another allowed source. To restrict, use a single rule.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/other -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 HTTP 200 OK ← 'other' also allowed — should be blocked!
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --from-label app=other --verdict FORWARDED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:01:01 other-xxx api-xxx FORWARDED ← Both rules permissive — additive, not sequential
$ kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -c fromEndpoints 2 ← Two ingress rules = both allowed (OR logic)
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 HTTP 200 OK ✅ Web allowed
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/other -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 wget: download timed out ✅ Other blocked

Debug Host Endpoint Policy — NodePort Traffic Blocked at Host Level

The Problem: You have a NodePort service on port 30080. External traffic times out. ClusterIP works internally. A Cilium host policy is blocking NodePort at the host level.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
kubectl expose deployment api -n anihpj --type=NodePort --port=80 --target-port=80

cat > ccnp-bug.yaml << 'CCNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: restrict-host
spec:
  nodeSelector: {matchLabels: {}}
  ingress:
  - fromEntities: [cluster]          # ❌ BUG: Only cluster — blocks world (external)!
CCNP
kubectl apply -f ccnp-bug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pod Running ✅, Service NodePort created ✅
✗2. ClusterIP works: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget http://api-service:80 → 200 OK ✅
✗3. NodePort blocked: curl http://NODE_IP:30080 → timeout ❌
✗4. Hubble host: hubble observe --from-entity world --to-port 30080 → DROPPED ❌
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check CCNP nodeSelector: kubectl get ccnp restrict-host -o yaml | grep -A5 nodeSelector
→ nodeSelector: {matchLabels: {}} — applies to ALL nodes!
2Check host policy entities: kubectl get ccnp restrict-host -o yaml | grep -A3 fromEntities
→ fromEntities: [cluster] — only cluster, NOT world!
3Understand NodePort traffic origin: External NodePort traffic comes from the 'world' entity — blocked by host policy

5Root cause identified: Host CCNP only allows 'cluster' entity — NodePort external traffic from 'world' entity is blocked

🔧 Fix — Add 'world' Entity to Host Policy for NodePort

BASH
kubectl delete ccnp restrict-host

apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-nodeport
spec:
  nodeSelector: {matchLabels: {}}
  ingress:
  - fromEntities: [world, cluster]
    toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "30080", protocol: TCP}]
CCNP
✓

✅ Verify — NodePort Accessible from External

After adding fromEntities: [world] to the host CCNP, external traffic to NodePort 30080 succeeds. curl http://NODE_IP:30080 returns HTTP 200. hubble observe shows FORWARDED from world entity. The key lesson: Host policies with nodeSelector control NodePort traffic — world entity must be allowed for external access.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
ClusterIP works → NodePort fails
②
Host policy restricts host
③
fromEntities: cluster only
④
world=external blocked
⑤
Add world → NodePort works

Tenet: Cilium host endpoint policies (using nodeSelector) control traffic TO the host including NodePort. NodePort traffic comes from world entity. If host policy only allows cluster, NodePort is blocked. Always include world for external access.

🧪 SCENARIO S35 — Category 2: Network Policy

Debug FQDN Policy — matchName vs matchPattern DNS Selector

The Problem: You used matchName: *.github.com expecting wildcard DNS matching, but traffic to api.github.com is DROPPED. Wildcards require matchPattern, not matchName.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-github
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: web}}
  egress:
  - toEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {k8s-app: kube-dns, "io.kubernetes.pod.namespace": kube-system}}]
    toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "53", protocol: UDP}]}]
  - toFQDNs:
    - matchName: "*.github.com"       # ❌ BUG: matchName = exact! Use matchPattern for wildcards
    toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "443", protocol: TCP}]}]
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pod Running ✅, DNS works (UDP/53 allowed) ✅
✗2. Traffic to api.github.com blocked: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget --timeout=3 https://api.github.com → timeout ❌
✗3. Hubble: DNS resolves api.github.com → 140.82.121.5 but traffic DROPPED ❌
✗4. Check FQDN rule: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep matchName → *.github.com
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Inspect FQDN rule type: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-github -o yaml | grep -E 'matchName|matchPattern'
→ matchName: "*.github.com" — matchName = exact match only!
2Understand matchName: matchName performs exact DNS name matching — "*.github.com" would need a literal DNS entry named *.github.com
3Understand matchPattern: matchPattern supports glob-style wildcards — "*.github.com" matches api.github.com, www.github.com, etc.
4Hubble confirms: hubble observe -n anihpj --verdict DROPPED → DNS resolves correctly but policy denies because matchName doesn't match
5Root cause identified: matchName = exact match — wildcards require matchPattern for pattern-based DNS matching
🔧

🔧 Fix — Change matchName to matchPattern for Wildcard DNS

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-github --type json -p '[{"op":"replace","path":"/spec/egress/1/toFQDNs/0/matchName","value":"*.github.com"},{"op":"remove","path":"/spec/egress/1/toFQDNs/0/matchName"},{"op":"add","path":"/spec/egress/1/toFQDNs/0/matchPattern","value":"*.github.com"}]' 2>/dev/null
# If patch fails, delete and re-create:
kubectl delete cnp -n anihpj allow-github
kubectl apply -f - << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-github
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: web}}
  egress:
  - toEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {k8s-app: kube-dns, "io.kubernetes.pod.namespace": kube-system}}]
    toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "53", protocol: UDP}]}]
  - toFQDNs:
    - matchPattern: "*.github.com"
    toPorts: [{ports: [{port: "443", protocol: TCP}]}]
CNP
✓

✅ Verify — All github.com Subdomains Now Allowed

After changing matchName to matchPattern, both api.github.com and www.github.com are reachable. hubble observe shows FORWARDED for all *.github.com traffic. The key lesson: matchName = exact DNS match, matchPattern = wildcard/glob match — use matchPattern for *.domain.com rules.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
FQDN wildcard blocked
②
matchName = exact match
③
matchPattern = wildcard
④
Change to matchPattern
⑤
Wildcard FQDN works

Tenet: matchName performs exact DNS name matching. For wildcard patterns like *.github.com, use matchPattern. matchPattern supports glob-style patterns: * matches any subdomain, ? matches single character.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 https://api.github.com wget: download timed out ← Wildcard not matching!
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --verdict DROPPED -o json | jq '.flow.destination_names' ["api.github.com"] ← DNS resolved correctly verdict: DROPPED ← But matchName expects literal *.github.com
$ kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -E 'matchName|matchPattern' matchName: *.github.com ← BUG: should be matchPattern!
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 https://api.github.com HTTP 200 OK ✅ Wildcard matchPattern works
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --verdict FORWARDED --to-fqdn github.com TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:08:01 web-xxx api.github.com:443 FORWARDED ✅
🧹

🧹 Cleanup

BASH
kubectl delete namespace anihpj

③
CNP says api-svc
④
Actual service = api-service
⑤

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: strict-api
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {app: web}}]
    toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "80", protocol: TCP}]
  egress:
  - toEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {app: web}}]
    toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "80", protocol: TCP}]
      # ❌ BUG: No ICMP or health entity rules!
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pod Running ✅, CNP applied ✅
✗2. Cilium health check failing: cilium status --brief → Controller Status: 16/17 healthy ❌
✗3. cilium-health unreachable: cilium-health status → unreachable ❌
✗4. Hubble ICMP: hubble observe --from-entity health --protocol ICMPv4 → DROPPED ❌
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check Cilium status: cilium status
→ Cluster health: Warning — cilium-health IP not reachable
2Hubble health entity: hubble observe --from-entity health --verdict DROPPED
→ All health probe traffic (ICMP + TCP/4240) is DROPPED
3Inspect CNP entities: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj strict-api -o yaml | grep -c health

🔧 Fix — Add 'health' Entity with ICMP and TCP/4240 Rules

BASH

  {"op":"add","path":"/spec/ingress/-","value":{"fromEntities":["health"],"toPorts":[{"ports":[{"port":"4240","protocol":"TCP"}]}]}}
]'
✓

After adding fromEntities: [health] with ICMP and TCP/4240 rules, cilium status shows 17/17 healthy with Cluster health Ok. The key lesson: always include the 'health' entity in strict policies — Cilium's own health checks depend on it.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Health checks failing
②
cilium status shows errors
③
ICMP from health blocked
④
No health entity allowed
⑤
Add health entity → checks pass

Tenet: Cilium uses cilium-health endpoints for internal connectivity verification. Strict policies that don't allow the health entity break Cilium's own health checks. Always include fromEntities: [health] with ICMP and TCP/4240 in your policies.

📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix

1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj

apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: baseline-egress
spec:
  endpointSelector: {}
  egress:

    - matchLabels: {}            # ❌ BUG: Only same-ns egress allowed!

③

④

⑤

🧪 SCENARIO S39 — Category 2: Network Policy

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine
# ❌ BUG: Pod labeled "webfront" but policy expects "web"!

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-from-web
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints:

✗1. Pods Running ✅, CNP exists ✅

✗3. Pod labels: kubectl get pods -n anihpj --show-labels → web pod has app=webfront
✗4. Policy is SILENT — no error, just drops traffic
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check pod labels: kubectl get pods -n anihpj --show-labels | grep web
→ app=webfront — pods have "webfront", NOT "web"!
2Check CNP selector: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj allow-from-web -o yaml | grep 'app:'
→ app: web — policy expects "web" but pods have "webfront"
3Hubble confirms mismatch: hubble observe -n anihpj --from-label app=webfront --verdict DROPPED → No policy matches "webfront"
4Silent deny: Cilium silently drops traffic when labels don't match — no error, no warning, no event
5Root cause identified: Label mismatch — pods have app=webfront but CNP expects app=web — single character difference causes silent deny
🔧

🔧 Fix — Align Pod Labels with CNP Selector

BASH
kubectl patch deployment web -n anihpj --type json -p '[{"op":"replace","path":"/spec/template/metadata/labels/app","value":"web"}]'
# OR update the policy:
# kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj allow-from-web --type merge -p '{"spec":{"ingress":[{"fromEndpoints":[{"matchLabels":{"app":"webfront"}}]}]}}'
✓

✅ Verify — Web Pods Now Match Policy Selector

After patching the deployment to use app: web, the CNP selector matches and traffic is allowed. hubble observe shows FORWARDED. The key lesson: Cilium policy selectors use exact label matching — a single typo causes a silent deny with zero error messages.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Traffic DROPPED → silent
②
Pod labels vs policy labels
③
app=webfront ≠ app=web
④
Label mismatch = deny
⑤
Align labels → works

Tenet: Cilium policy selectors use exact label matching. A single character difference (web vs webfront) causes a silent deny with no error messages. Always verify pod labels with kubectl get pods --show-labels and compare against policy selectors.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl get pods -n anihpj --show-labels | grep web web-xxx 1/1 Running app=webfront,pod-template-hash=... ← app=webfront!
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 wget: download timed out ← Silent deny — no error, just drop
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --from-label app=webfront --verdict DROPPED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:01:01 web-xxx api-xxx DROPPED ← Policy expects app=web, not webfront
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ kubectl get pods -n anihpj --show-labels | grep web web-xxx 1/1 Running app=web,pod-template-hash=... ✅ Labels aligned
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 HTTP 200 OK ✅ Selector now matches
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --from-label app=web --to-label app=api --verdict FORWARDED TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:05:01 web-xxx api-xxx FORWARDED ✅
🧹

🧹 Cleanup

BASH
kubectl delete namespace anihpj
S40
🧪 SCENARIO S40 — Category 2: Network Policy

Debug Combined Policy — Ingress Port Different from Container Port

The Problem: You created a CNP with both ingress and egress rules. Ingress allows port 8080 but egress only allows port 443. The API pod can receive requests but can't respond — one-way communication only.
1

📋 Deploy the YAML (contains the bug)

BASH
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl create deployment api -n anihpj --image=nginx:alpine    # Listens on 80
kubectl create deployment web -n anihpj --image=busybox -- sleep 3600

cat > cnbug.yaml << 'CNP'
apiVersion: cilium.io/v2
kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: asymmetric-policy
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  endpointSelector: {matchLabels: {app: api}}
  ingress:
  - fromEndpoints: [{matchLabels: {app: web}}]
    toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "80", protocol: TCP}]       # Ingress OK on 80
  egress:
  - toPorts:
    - ports: [{port: "443", protocol: TCP}]      # ❌ BUG: Egress only 443 — can't respond on 80!
CNP
kubectl apply -f cnbug.yaml
⚠

⚠️ Observe the Error

✗1. Pods Running ✅, CNP exists ✅
✗2. Web→api request SENT: kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget --timeout=3 http://api-ip:80 → timeout ❌
✗3. Hubble: request FORWARDED on ingress, but response DROPPED on egress ❌
✗4. One-way communication: packets arrive at API but responses are blocked
🔍

🔍 Debug — Find the Root Cause

1Check ingress rules: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj asymmetric-policy -o yaml | grep -A10 ingress
→ port 80 TCP allowed — ingress direction is correct
2Check egress rules: kubectl get cnp -n anihpj asymmetric-policy -o yaml | grep -A10 egress
→ Only port 443 TCP allowed — response on port 80 is BLOCKED!
3Hubble confirms asymmetric: hubble observe -n anihpj -o json | jq 'select(.flow.l4.TCP.destination_port==80)'
→ ingress:FORWARDED, reply:egress:DROPPED — TCP handshake fails
4Understand bidirectionality: Cilium evaluates ingress and egress independently — both directions need allow rules
5Root cause identified: Asymmetric policy — ingress allows port 80 but egress only allows 443, blocking HTTP responses
🔧

🔧 Fix — Add Cluster Entity to Egress for Bidirectional Traffic

BASH
kubectl patch cnp -n anihpj asymmetric-policy --type json -p '[{"op":"add","path":"/spec/egress/-","value":{"toEntities":["cluster"]}}]'
✓

✅ Verify — Full Bidirectional Communication Works

After adding toEntities: [cluster] to egress, HTTP responses on port 80 can leave the API pod. wget from web succeeds. hubble observe shows FORWARDED in both directions. The key lesson: Cilium evaluates ingress and egress independently — asymmetric allow creates one-way communication.

🧠 Diagnostic Tenet (Thought Process)
①
Request sent → no response
②
Ingress:80 OK, Egress:443 only
③
Response on :80 blocked
④
Asymmetric policy
⑤
Add cluster egress → bidirectional

Tenet: Cilium evaluates ingress and egress rules independently. If ingress allows port 80 but egress doesn't, the TCP SYN arrives but the SYN-ACK response is dropped. Always match egress rules to expected response ports, or use toEntities: [cluster] for unrestricted responses.

📟 Command Outputs — Error State (BEFORE fix)
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 wget: download timed out ← Request sent, response blocked
$ hubble observe -n anihpj -o json | jq 'select(.flow.l4.TCP.destination_port==80)' "ingress": "FORWARDED", "egress": "DROPPED" ← Asymmetric: ingress OK, egress denied
$ kubectl get cnp -n anihpj -o yaml | grep -A3 'egress:' egress: - toPorts: - ports: [{port: "443", protocol: TCP}] ← Only 443 egress — response on :80 blocked!
📟 Command Outputs — AFTER Fix
$ kubectl exec -n anihpj deploy/web -- wget -qO- --timeout=3 http://10.0.1.20:80 HTTP 200 OK ✅ Bidirectional traffic works
$ hubble observe -n anihpj --from-label app=web --to-label app=api TIMESTAMP SOURCE DESTINATION VERDICT 12:05:01 web-xxx api-xxx:80 FORWARDED ✅ 12:05:01 api-xxx web-xxx:45812 FORWARDED ✅
🧹

🧹 Cleanup

BASH
kubectl delete namespace anihpj
